::: {#A5.p3 .ltx_para}
We also tested on the Open-QA benchmarks NQ, TQ, and SQuAD, each of
which has approximately 9k dev-set questions and muli-hop HoVer, whose
development set has 4k claims. In the compression
evaluation §[[B]{.ltx_text
.ltx_ref_tag}](#A2 "Appendix B Impact of Compression ‣ ColBERTv2:
Effective and Efficient Retrieval via Lightweight Late Interaction"){.ltx_ref},
we used models trained in-domain on NQ and HoVer, whose training sets
contain 79k and 18k queries, respectively.
:::
::::::

:::::::::::: {#A6 .section .ltx_appendix}
## [Appendix F ]{.ltx_tag .ltx_tag_appendix}Implementation & Hyperparameters {#appendix-f-implementation-hyperparameters .ltx_title .ltx_title_appendix}

::: {#A6.p1 .ltx_para}
We implement ColBERTv2 using Python 3.7, PyTorch 1.9, and HuggingFace
Transformers 4.10 [Wolf et al. 2020](#bib.bib65){.ltx_ref}, extending
the original implementation of ColBERT by [Khattab and Zaharia
2020](#bib.bib33){.ltx_ref}. We use FAISS 1.7 [Johnson et al.
2019](#bib.bib27){.ltx_ref} for $k$-means clustering,[^9^[[^9^
[9]{.ltx_tag .ltx_tag_note}
[https://github.com/facebookresearch/faiss](https://github.com/facebookresearch/faiss){.ltx_ref
.ltx_url
.ltx_font_typewriter}]{.ltx_note_content}]{.ltx_note_outer}]{#footnote9
.ltx_note .ltx_role_footnote} though unlike ColBERT we do not use it for
nearest-neighbor search. Instead, we implement our candidate generation
mechanism (§[[3.5]{.ltx_text
.ltx_ref_tag}](#S3.SS5 "3.5 Retrieval ‣ 3 ColBERTv2 ‣ ColBERTv2:
Effective and Efficient Retrieval via Lightweight Late Interaction"){.ltx_ref})
using PyTorch primitives in Python.
:::

::: {#A6.p2 .ltx_para}
We conducted our experiments on an internal cluster, typically using up
to four 12GB Titan V GPUs for each of the inference tasks (e.g.,
indexing, computing distillation scores, and retrieval) and four 80GB
A100 GPUs for training, though GPUs with smaller RAM can be used via
gradient accumulation. Using this infrastructure, computing the
distillation scores takes under a day, training a 64-way model on MS
MARCO for 400,000 steps takes around five days, and indexing takes
approximately two hours. We very roughly estimate an upper bound total
of 20 GPU-months for all experimentation, development, and evaluation
performed for this work over a period of several months.
:::

::: {#A6.p3 .ltx_para}
Like ColBERT, our encoder is a [bert-base-uncased]{#A6.p3.1.1 .ltx_text
.ltx_font_typewriter} model that is shared between the query and passage
encoders and which has 110M parameters. We retain the default vector
dimension suggested by [Khattab and Zaharia 2020](#bib.bib33){.ltx_ref}
and used in subsequent work, namely, $d$=128. For the experiments
reported in this paper, we train on MS MARCO training set. We use simple
defaults with limited manual exploration on the official development set
for the learning rate ($10^{-5}$), batch size (32 examples), and warm up
(for 20,000 steps) with linear decay.
:::

::: {#A6.p4 .ltx_para}
Hyperparameters corresponding to retrieval are explored
in §[[C]{.ltx_text
.ltx_ref_tag}](#A3 "Appendix C Retrieval Latency ‣ ColBERTv2:
Effective and Efficient Retrieval via Lightweight Late Interaction"){.ltx_ref}.
We default to $\texttt{probe}=2$, but use $\texttt{probe}=4$ on the
largest datasets, namely, MS MARCO and Wikipedia. By default we set
$\texttt{candidates}=\texttt{probe}*2^{12}$, but for Wikipedia we set
$\texttt{candidates}=\texttt{probe}*2^{13}$ and for MS MARCO we set
$\texttt{candidates}=\texttt{probe}*2^{14}$. We leave extensive tuning
of hyperparameters to future work.
:::

::: {#A6.p5 .ltx_para}
We train on MS MARCO using 64-way tuples for distillation, sampling them
from the top-500 retrieved passages per query. The training set of MS
MARCO contains approximately 800k queries, though only about 500k have
associated labels. We apply distillation using all 800k queries, where
each training example contains exactly one "positive", defined as a
passage labeled as positive or the top-ranked passage by the
cross-encoder teacher, irrespective of its label.
:::

::: {#A6.p6 .ltx_para}
We train for 400k steps, initializing from a pre-finetuned checkpoint
using 32-way training examples and 150k steps. To generate the top-$k$
passages per training query, we apply two rounds, following [Khattab
et al. 2021b](#bib.bib32){.ltx_ref}. We start from a model trained with
hard triples (akin to [Khattab et al. 2021b](#bib.bib32){.ltx_ref}),
train with distillation, and then use the distilled model to retrieve
for the second round of training. Preliminary experiments indicate that
quality has low sensitivity to this initialization and two-round
training, suggesting that both of them could be avoided to reduce the
cost of training.
:::

::: {#A6.p7 .ltx_para}
Unless otherwise stated, the results shown represent a single run. The
latency results in §[[3]{.ltx_text
.ltx_ref_tag}](#A2.F3 "Figure 3 ‣ Appendix B Impact of Compression ‣ ColBERTv2:
Effective and Efficient Retrieval via Lightweight Late Interaction"){.ltx_ref}
are averages of three runs. To evaluate for Open-QA retrieval, we use
evaluation scripts from [Khattab et al. 2021b](#bib.bib32){.ltx_ref},
which checks if the short answer string appears in the (titled)
Wikipedia passage. This adapts the DPR [Karpukhin et al.
2020](#bib.bib29){.ltx_ref} evaluation code.[^10^[[^10^ [10]{.ltx_tag
.ltx_tag_note}
[https://github.com/facebookresearch/DPR/blob/main/dpr/data/qa_validation.py](https://github.com/facebookresearch/DPR/blob/main/dpr/data/qa_validation.py){.ltx_ref
.ltx_url
.ltx_font_typewriter}]{.ltx_note_content}]{.ltx_note_outer}]{#footnote10
.ltx_note .ltx_role_footnote} We use the preprocessed Wikipedia Dec 2018
dump released by [Karpukhin et al. 2020](#bib.bib29){.ltx_ref}.
:::

::: {#A6.p8 .ltx_para}
For out-of-domain evaluation, we elected to follow [Thakur et al.
2021](#bib.bib58){.ltx_ref} and set the maximum document length of
ColBERT, RocketQAv2, and ColBERTv2 to 300 tokens on BEIR and LoTTE.
[Formal et al. 2021a](#bib.bib14){.ltx_ref} selected maximum sequence
length 256 for SPLADEv2 both on MS MARCO and on BEIR for both queries
and documents, and we retained this default when testing their system on
LoTTE. Unless otherwise stated, we keep the default query maximum
sequence length for ColBERTv2 and RocketQAv2, which is 32 tokens. For
the ArguAna test in BEIR, as the queries are themselves long documents,
we set the maximum query length used by ColBERTv2 and RocketQAv2 to 300.
For Climate-FEVER, as the queries are relatively long sentence claims,
we set the maximum query length used by ColBERTv2 to 64.
:::

::: {#A6.p9 .ltx_para}
We use the open source BEIR implementation[^11^[[^11^ [11]{.ltx_tag
.ltx_tag_note}
[https://github.com/UKPLab/beir](https://github.com/UKPLab/beir){.ltx_ref
.ltx_url
.ltx_font_typewriter}]{.ltx_note_content}]{.ltx_note_outer}]{#footnote11
.ltx_note .ltx_role_footnote} and SPLADEv2 evaluation[^12^[[^12^
[12]{.ltx_tag .ltx_tag_note}
[https://github.com/naver/splade](https://github.com/naver/splade){.ltx_ref
.ltx_url
.ltx_font_typewriter}]{.ltx_note_content}]{.ltx_note_outer}]{#footnote12
.ltx_note .ltx_role_footnote} code as the basis for our evaluations of
SPLADEv2 and ANCE as well as for BM25 on LoTTE. We use the
Anserini [Yang et al. 2018a](#bib.bib70){.ltx_ref} toolkit for BM25 on
the Wikipedia Open-QA retrieval tests as in [Khattab et al.
2021b](#bib.bib32){.ltx_ref}. We use the implementation developed by the
RocketQAv2 authors for evaluating RocketQAv2.[^13^[[^13^ [13]{.ltx_tag
.ltx_tag_note}
[https://github.com/PaddlePaddle/RocketQA](https://github.com/PaddlePaddle/RocketQA){.ltx_ref
.ltx_url
.ltx_font_typewriter}]{.ltx_note_content}]{.ltx_note_outer}]{#footnote13
.ltx_note .ltx_role_footnote}
:::

## Instructions for reporting errors

We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback
helps enhance accessibility and mobile support. To report errors in the
HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of
the methods listed below:

- Click the \"Report Issue\"
  [(![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyByb2xlPSJwcmVzZW50YXRpb24iIHN0eWxlPSJkaXNwbGF5OiBpbmxpbmUtYmxvY2s7IHZlcnRpY2FsLWFsaWduOiBtaWRkbGU7IGZpbGw6IHZhcigtLXRleHQtY29sb3IpOyIgYXJpYS1oaWRkZW49InRydWUiIGhlaWdodD0iMWVtIiB2aWV3Ym94PSIwIDAgNjQwIDY0MCI+CiAgICAgICAgICAgIDxwYXRoIGQ9Ik0yMjQgMTYwQzIyNCAxMDcgMjY3IDY0IDMyMCA2NEMzNzMgNjQgNDE2IDEwNyA0MTYgMTYwTDQxNiAxNjMuNkM0MTYgMTc5LjMgNDAzLjMgMTkyIDM4Ny42IDE5MkwyNTIuNSAxOTJDMjM2LjggMTkyIDIyNC4xIDE3OS4zIDIyNC4xIDE2My42TDIyNC4xIDE2MHpNNTY5LjYgMTcyLjhDNTgwLjIgMTg2LjkgNTc3LjMgMjA3IDU2My4yIDIxNy42TDQ2NS40IDI5MC45QzQ3MC43IDI5OS44IDQ3NC43IDMwOS42IDQ3Ny4yIDMyMEw1NzYgMzIwQzU5My43IDMyMCA2MDggMzM0LjMgNjA4IDM1MkM2MDggMzY5LjcgNTkzLjcgMzg0IDU3NiAzODRMNDgwIDM4NEw0ODAgNDE2QzQ4MCA0MTguNiA0NzkuOSA0MjEuMyA0NzkuOCA0MjMuOUw1NjMuMiA0ODYuNEM1NzcuMyA0OTcgNTgwLjIgNTE3LjEgNTY5LjYgNTMxLjJDNTU5IDU0NS4zIDUzOC45IDU0OC4yIDUyNC44IDUzNy42TDQ2MS43IDQ5MC4zQzQzOC41IDUzNC41IDM5NS4yIDU2Ni41IDM0NCA1NzQuMkwzNDQgMzQ0QzM0NCAzMzAuNyAzMzMuMyAzMjAgMzIwIDMyMEMzMDYuNyAzMjAgMjk2IDMzMC43IDI5NiAzNDRMMjk2IDU3NC4yQzI0NC44IDU2Ni41IDIwMS41IDUzNC41IDE3OC4zIDQ5MC4zTDExNS4yIDUzNy42QzEwMS4xIDU0OC4yIDgxIDU0NS4zIDcwLjQgNTMxLjJDNTkuOCA1MTcuMSA2Mi43IDQ5NyA3Ni44IDQ4Ni40TDE2MC4yIDQyMy45QzE2MC4xIDQyMS4zIDE2MCA0MTguNyAxNjAgNDE2TDE2MCAzODRMNjQgMzg0QzQ2LjMgMzg0IDMyIDM2OS43IDMyIDM1MkMzMiAzMzQuMyA0Ni4zIDMyMCA2NCAzMjBMMTYyLjggMzIwQzE2NS4zIDMwOS42IDE2OS4zIDI5OS44IDE3NC42IDI5MC45TDc2LjggMjE3LjZDNjIuNyAyMDcgNTkuOCAxODYuOSA3MC40IDE3Mi44QzgxIDE1OC43IDEwMS4xIDE1NS44IDExNS4yIDE2Ni40TDIyNCAyNDhDMjM2LjMgMjQyLjkgMjQ5LjggMjQwIDI2NCAyNDBMMzc2IDI0MEMzOTAuMiAyNDAgNDAzLjcgMjQyLjggNDE2IDI0OEw1MjQuOCAxNjYuNEM1MzguOSAxNTUuOCA1NTkgMTU4LjcgNTY5LjYgMTcyLjh6IiAvPgogICAgICAgICAgPC9zdmc+))]{.mobile-only}

**Tip:** You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your
report.

Our team has already identified [the following
issues](https://github.com/arXiv/html_feedback/issues){.ltx_ref
target="_blank"}. We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting
rendering errors we may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us
improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability should not
be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support
in championing open access for all.

Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our
collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a [list of packages that need
conversion](https://github.com/brucemiller/LaTeXML/wiki/Porting-LaTeX-packages-for-LaTeXML){.ltx_ref
target="_blank"}, and welcome [developer
contributions](https://github.com/brucemiller/LaTeXML/issues){.ltx_ref
target="_blank"}.
